    "defectId": "envelope-mangled-in-pryzm-3d-vs-cesium",
    "rootCauseFound": true,
    "rootCause": "The PRYZM 3D (three.js) view draws a `spaceEnvelope` through exactly ONE path — `SpaceEnvelopeMeshBuilder._faceGeometry` (apps/editor/src/engine/SpaceEnvelopeMeshBuilder.ts:443-488), constructed once in `attachSpaceEnvelopeRender` (:138) and attached once in `initTools.ts:2147-2149` onto `world.scene.three`, whose transform is pinned to identity (`packages/core-app-model/src/BimWorld.ts:168-170`). That builder emits the n side faces as explicit quads (correct for any ring) but emits the TOP and BOTTOM caps as a NAIVE TRIANGLE FAN FROM VERTEX 0 (`for (let i = 1; i < n - 1; i += 1) { p0 = ring[0]; p1 = ring[i]; p2 = ring[i+1]; ... }`, :477-482). A vertex-0 fan is only a valid triangulation of a CONVEX ring — the file says so itself at :467-474 (\"Correct for a CONVEX ring only … it is not done here because the ring vocabulary this family authors today is a rectangle\"). The Cesium path (`CesiumViewport.renderSpaceEnvelopes`, :8892-8911) hands the SAME ring, in the same order, to `new Cesium.PolygonHierarchy(positions)` with `height`/`extrudedHeight`, and Cesium triangulates with earcut (bundled: node_modules/cesium/ThirdParty.json:60), which is correct for any simple concave ring. So the two views consume identical data and differ in exactly one algorithm: the cap triangulation. The precondition the fan declared has since been broken by the authoring routes that shipped AFTER it: the fan landed 2026-09-04 (678e744f) while the \"extrude the permitted buildable footprint\" route landed 2026-09-06 (9d4ee67f, `parcelLawEnvelopeAuthoring.ts:424` → `env.insetPolygon`, the very ring the founder's own console calls \"the 20-corner buildable footprint\", CesiumViewport.ts:8700-8701) and the free-draw surface landed 2026-09-07 (bea53747, modes including freehand polyline / arcs). ISSUE-LOG L-428 already records in the founder's own project that \"a buildable-envelope inset is an irregular/concave polygon (founder's case: 12-vertex)\". `envelopeAuthoringPlan.ts:628-629` copies that one ring verbatim into all six storey records, so every one of the 6 level prisms gets 2 wrong caps. Measured on a simple 12-corner L-shaped plot (script run this session): 11 of the 12 possible ring start-vertices produce a wrong cap, with up to 276 m² of fan triangles lying OUTSIDE a 552 m² footprint. Twelve such caps (6 storeys × top+bottom) at different heights, drawn `transparent` + `DoubleSide` + `depthWrite:false` (:323-340), is exactly the founder's \"folded/twisted sheet with spikes and self-intersecting purple wedges\", sitting inside a side-face \"fence\" that is geometrically correct.",

    "proposedFix": "Replace the vertex-0 fan in `SpaceEnvelopeMeshBuilder._faceGeometry`'s TOP/BOTTOM arm (apps/editor/src/engine/SpaceEnvelopeMeshBuilder.ts:475-487) with the real triangulator the file's own comment names, reusing the convention already established 100 lines away in ParcelBoundarySceneRenderer: build a `THREE.Shape` as `moveTo(ring[0].x, -ring[0].z)` + `lineTo(p.x, -p.z)` + `closePath()`, take `new THREE.ShapeGeometry(shape)` (earcut — the same triangulator Cesium uses, so the two views agree by construction), then `geo.rotateX(-Math.PI / 2)` and `geo.translate(0, y, 0)`. ⛔ The sign is load-bearing: `-Math.PI/2` maps (u, v, 0) → (u, 0, −v), which with v = −p.z returns exactly (p.x, y, p.z); `+Math.PI/2` is the §PARCEL-SHADE-NOT-MIRRORED defect (L-10740) and would mirror the cap about the scene X axis. Wrap in try/catch and fall back to the existing fan (never to nothing), matching `buildFill`'s \"returns null if triangulation fails\" posture. Do NOT change anything else: each cap stays ONE mesh carrying its own `userData.spaceEnvelopeFace` (`{kind:'top'}` / `{kind:'bottom'}`), so the face-index convention, the drag controller, the gizmo and `spaceEnvelope.moveFace` are untouched — only the triangle list inside the cap changes. Add the missing red-first test (there is no SpaceEnvelopeMeshBuilder cap test today): on a concave L-shaped ring, assert (1) every cap triangle's centroid satisfies `pointInRing` from `@pryzm/geometry-space-envelope`, and (2) the summed cap triangle area equals `footprintAreaM2(ring)` within epsilon. Both fail on the current fan and pass on ShapeGeometry; rotate the ring's start vertex across all n positions in the test, because the fan happens to be correct for a start vertex inside the polygon's kernel (1 of 12 rotations passed in my measurement) and a single-rotation test would be a false green. SECONDARY, not the root cause but worth filing: (a) `ringSelfIntersects` exists in the geometry package but is only enforced on face-move (packages/geometry-space-envelope/src/SpaceEnvelopeFaceMove.ts:323) — creation from a freehand-drawn ring has no such guard, so a bow-tie perimeter would mangle BOTH views; (b) on a 20-corner footprint `SpaceEnvelopeFaceGizmoBuilder` puts n+2 = 22 double-headed cone arrows on the hovered envelope, which will read as additional \"spikes\" in a screenshot even once the caps are fixed.",

(4) `BimWorld.ts:168-170` pins the scene root to identity, killing the transform/theta rival — and a theta error rotates rigidly, it cannot fold a cap. (5) Chronology exact: `678e744f` 2026-09-04 (the fan), `9d4ee67f` 2026-09-06 (extrude the permitted inset), `bea53747` 2026-09-07 (draw surface). In the test, the summed-area assertion is the load-bearing one — a centroid-only check passes at start-vertex 0 on a fixture where the fan overstates the cap by 180 m2."

Once per tab is TRUE IN PRACTICE, though the claim's cited authority is contradicted by the file it is diagnosing: `GISAreaLayout.ts:126` says \"mountGISArea can run more than once in a session (project switch)\". The code settles it: one call site (`Layout.ts:92` ← `initUI.ts:3301`) behind `src/main.ts:202 let _bootstrapped = false` — bootstrap runs once per tab, and the hub is re-entered in-SPA (`PlatformRouter.showHub`), no reload.\n4. Unconditional teardown, no reseed. `clearLayoutProjectState` (`:8479`) runs `envelopeFaceDrag3dDispose?.()` (`:8504`) and `envelopeFaceDrag3dUnregister?.()` (`:8509`); the scope registration (`:156-161`) declares only `clear`. Both routes reach it: `siteProjectScope.ts:287 clearScopes(GIS_SWITCH_SCOPES)` (derived from DECLARED_PROJECT_SCOPES, and `gis.areaLayout` is declared with a different owning module and no `ownsTeardown:false`, so it is included) and `ClearProjectCommand.ts:246 clearAll()`.\n5. The DRAW asymmetry is real: `:8498` only calls `disarm()`; `envelopeDraw3dUnregister` is never called there, and `arm()` rebuilds its ScreenSpaceEventHandler on the still-live viewer.\n6. The founder's documented behaviour hits the deterministic path. `docs/03-execution/plans/SESSION-HANDOVER-2026-09-08.md §3`: he \"opened a brand-new empty project\" with console attached, in a tab that already had one — that is exactly \"second project in one tab\". The same trace (`[space-envelope] drew 15/15 authored envelope(s)`) also proves a frame WAS seated at that moment, which independently closes the frame-gate rival for that session. And the feature is deployed: `c8c62c51` (09-07 16:04) is an ancestor of HEAD and 09-08 shipped builds on top of it, so this is not a recurrence of L-13236 #1 (\"not in any build he has run\") — the rival I considered most likely given this repo's history.\n\nRIVALS I CONSTRUCTED AND KILLED: re-parenting (a DOM move keeps listeners); source-of-truth divergence (`renderSpaceEnvelopes` reads `runtime.stores.spaceEnvelope`, the identical store the pick reads); role refusal (parcel-law create mints `role:'level'`, which is draggable, and `maximumBuildable` refuses OUT LOUD); focus id mismatch (`storeyRows()` takes ids from `readLevelEnvelopes` → the store's own `r.id`); vertical frame mismatch (rasteriser `bottom = baseHeight + baseOffset` is the exact inverse of the pick's `y = o.z - baseHeightM`); the THREE-canvas overlay swallowing pointers (Cesium's own draw handler binds the SAME `viewer.scene.canvas`, so draw and drag live or die together); and viewer dispose+re-mount (it would break the DRAW too, since `SiteEnvelopeDrawCesium` captures the viewer by value).\n\nWHERE THE CLAIM IS WRONG OR OVERSTATED:\n(a) Its two orderings are mis-weighted. Case (2) (onboarding race) is WEAKER than presented: `ensureGisInitialized('eager')` is gated on `consumeEagerGlobeStart()`, and its own comment states \"a hub project open (no onboarding) never consumes the flag\". On the hub path the init runs in 'activation' mode — only at the user's first 3-D Site activation (`:1499`), which is necessarily AFTER `setProjectContext` and after `ProjectLoader.load`'s `clearAll`. So on a hub-opened FIRST project the gesture is ALIVE, and read as \"always dead\" the claim is false. Case (1) is the load-bearing one, and it holds.\n(b) Its dismissal of hypothesis (b) is logically unsound even though the conclusion survives: `clearSpaceEnvelopes()` is called only by `dispose()` (`CesiumViewport.ts:18954`) and by `renderSpaceEnvelopes` itself — NOT by `resetProjectScopedState('project-switch')`, which nulls `formaMassingOrigin` at `:18640`. Prisms already drawn therefore stay on screen while the frame read returns null. \"Visible\" and \"a frame exists now\" are reads at different times and CAN disagree; the argument \"they read the same field so they cannot disagree\" is wrong.\n(c) One silent rival is never analysed: `SiteEnvelopeDrawCesium.pickFace` returns `null` whenever `this.sink !== null` (an armed perimeter draw, `siteEnvelopeDrawCesium.ts:737`), and `cannotDragReason()` does NOT check `sink` — so an armed draw makes the gesture deaf while the panel's Drag face button stays LIVE. That state is precisely what the author's own falsification test would misread as \"diagnosis falsified\". It self-heals (`takeSpaceEnvelopeFaceDragFocus` disarms the draw) and `finish()` disarms, so it is narrower than the teardown — but it belongs in the probe's interpretation, not only in its output list.\n(d) Minor: `reseedAll()` is at `ClearProjectCommand.ts:251`, not `:249` (249 is the failure log).\n\nVERDICT: the claimed defect exists exactly as described and is severe (permanent for the tab, no recovery), the founder's known workflow reaches it, and no simpler explanation survived. What is NOT established is causation in the specific failing session — the author's own console test remains unrun, and the fix should not be shipped without the probe, because (b) and (c) can produce the identical symptom from the same click.",

   "defect": "globe-seat-too-high",
   "finding": {
    "defectId": "GLOBE-MODEL-FLOATS-~13m-ABOVE-PHOTOREAL-TILES (§GLOBE-SLOPE-SEAT, regression of L-12919 / commit c36b75bc)",
    "rootCauseFound": true,
    "rootCause": "CONFIRMED, and the arithmetic reproduces the founder's console exactly. `reduceTileGroundHeight` seats the whole globe scene on `classifyTileGroundPicks`, a discriminator with exactly two thresholds applied to a SORTED 1-D list of heights (globeGroundAnchor.ts:198-216): span = median − p10 > 6 m rules out `plateau`; largest consecutive step in the low half > 4 m rules out `roof-gap`; everything else falls through to `slope`, which seats at the ring MEDIAN (`:279`). The founder's ring is 58 picks, span 12.59 m, largest step 2.00 m — so it takes the `slope` arm, and seat = median 78.94 − GLOBE_GROUND_SEAT_EPSILON_M 0.30 = 78.64 m, which is byte-for-byte the console's `-> seat 78.64`. The lowest credible pick is 64.89 m, so the building, the massing and the white void cap are all seated 13.75 m above the lowest measured street cell — dead centre of the reported 10-14 m. Under the pre-L-12919 rule the seat would have been p10 − 0.30 = 66.05 m, i.e. flush; this defect is a three-day-old regression from commit c36b75bc (2026-09-05, the Sète hillside fix). WHY THE DISCRIMINATOR CANNOT WORK, ARCHITECTURALLY: `safeSampleTileHeights` (CesiumViewport.ts:9014-9036) compacts the sampler result into a bare `number[]`, discarding the (lon,lat) of every pick before the classifier ever runs. Slope and roofs differ only in whether height is a FUNCTION OF POSITION; once position is thrown away the two are genuinely indistinguishable, and no threshold on a sorted list can recover it. NEGATIVE: drop any pick whose (lon,lat) falls inside a known context building footprint buffered ~3 m outward (to catch the mesh's facade drape). POSITIVE: prefer picks lying on an OSM road centreline / within its carriageway half-width; `loadContextRoads` (:13718) already holds the ways. Seat = low quantile of the surviving picks. The slope/plateau arms then become unnecessary: they exist only because ground and roof were indistinguishable.\n\n(2) SECOND ARM — bare-earth cross-check, independent source, SAME datum. On the globe path `maybeAttachTerrainProvider` runs (:5250) and `contextGroundCache` already holds `sampleTerrainMostDetailed` heights; the baked terrain is ellipsoidal WGS-84 (terrain.mjs bakes the napToEllipsoidal geoid lift — see the maybeAttachTerrainProvider header at :10984ff). Compute r_i = pick_i − terrain(lat_i,lon_i). Ground picks cluster at r ≈ c (a constant tile-vs-terrain bias); roofs sit at c + storeys. Set c = low quantile of r so ANY constant datum bias cancels, then classify ground as r_i − c <= ~2.5 m. This is immune to the ADR-0268 §156 / L-142 objection because terrain is used as SHAPE only — the seat still comes from the photoreal picks themselves, never from terrain.\n\n(3) FALLBACK ARM — fit a plane, do not sort a list. Least-squares h ≈ a·east + b·north + c, trimmed to the lower envelope (fit, drop picks >2.5 m above the plane, refit, 3x). Declare `slope` only when the inliers cover >= ~60% of picks AND residuals are symmetric AND |grad h| = hypot(a,b) is in a plausible terrain range. Seat at the plane evaluated AT THE PARCEL CENTROID — not the ring median. That is the correct estimator on a real hillside too (a ring median equals the centre value only for a symmetric ring and a linear slope), so this collapses both arms into one and fixes Sète more correctly than L-12919 did. A one-sided POSITIVE residual tail is itself the roof signature; print its skew.\n\n(4) RELATIVE CEILING (cheap, ship first). Whatever arm wins, refuse a seat above min_credible + |grad h|·R_ring. Replace the synthetic bimodal fixture (GlobeGroundAnchor.test.ts:473-480) with the REAL 58-value Barcelona pick set captured from the founder's session. Add the Sète ring as the second real corpus. Corrections to the claim: (a) scope it to the design, not 'the whole globe scene' — context buildings seat off contextGroundCache/sampleGround (:10419, :10436), a separate datum, which is itself worth checking since a design-vs-context datum mismatch is a second candidate for 'floats above the city'; (b) drop the 'facade drape' mechanism — clampToHeightMostDetailed rays go straight DOWN from 1 km (:9337) and cannot hit a vertical wall, so the continuum comes from photoreal mesh reconstruction smearing roof edges into ramps plus trees/awnings/overhangs; the measured 2.00 m step is the evidence, the facade story is not; (c) do NOT ship fix (4) first — its 64.89 + 0.02*60 arithmetic assumes the flat-site conclusion, and on a genuine slope the ring's own span implies grad ~0.21 and a ceiling of ~76.9 m that would fail to reject 78.64, so the relative ceiling is only meaningful once arm 2 or arm 3 supplies an independently measured gradient; (d) in the falsification recipe, note that the DIVERGED flag reports LTP-vs-store-address, while anchor<->LTP is printed unflagged — read the metre value, not the flag. Sequencing: make safeSampleTileHeights carry {lat, lon, h} (prerequisite 0), then log the 58 picks with position plus their footprint-membership and baked-terrain residual. That one log decides between 'roof-heavy ring' and 'real hillside' and is the only thing standing between a confirmed mechanism and a confirmed root cause.",
    "Not a replacement — a surviving rival plus two corrections that change the FIX, and one cheaper fix the claim missed.\n\nSURVIVING RIVAL (untested by the author, and it decides whether their fix works): the ring may be so roof-heavy that even the p10 is NOT street. Placa Sant Jaume is medieval fabric with 3-6 m streets; two 16-point compass rings at ext+25 / ext+55 plus 1.7x-pushed vertices may hit almost no open street cell, and Google's mesh webs across narrow streets. The claim asserts the pre-L-12919 seat (p10 - 0.30 = 66.05) \"would be flush\" purely because it is lower — there is no evidence it is ground. Note the repo's own earlier Barcelona reading (L-479 comment: \"picks=48 -> 50.87 m (correct: Barcelona tile surface)\") is ~14 m BELOW the quoted min of 64.89; different parcel is the likely explanation, but it is unverified, and if it were the same parcel the ENTIRE ring is elevated and no quantile of it fixes anything. Reverting to p10 therefore risks the §SAME-RULE-TWO-IMPLEMENTATIONS / §COMMITTED-IS-NOT-REACHABLE outcome: shipped, tests green, founder still sees the float.\n\nTWO FIX PREMISES ARE FALSE ON THIS EXACT PATH:\n- Arm (1) \"the data is already in memory — contextBuildingPlacements\": FALSE. CesiumViewport.ts:8301-8312 aborts the fetch and calls clearContextBuildings() whenever `keepPhotoreal && photorealTilesActive`. There are no footprints on the photoreal globe. The arm needs a new data-only footprint fetch first.\n- Arm (2) \"maybeAttachTerrainProvider runs (:5250) and contextGroundCache already holds heights\": FALSE. decideBakedTerrainAttach returns {attach:false, reason:'photoreal'} for photorealActive && !formaMode, so no provider is attached and no terrain heights exist to difference against. The arm needs its own fetch, and it leans harder on ADR-0268 §156 than the proposal admits.\nArms (4) (relative ceiling min_credible + |grad h|·R_ring) and (5) (replace the synthetic fixtures with the real 58-value Barcelona set and the Sète ring) are sound, cheap, and would have caught BOTH Sète and this. Ship (4) and (5) first.\n\nTHE CHEAPER FIX NEITHER SIDE PROPOSED: the seat must estimate the ground UNDER the parcel, but §GLOBE-DONT-MEASURE-THE-HOLE forfeits every in-parcel pick because we clip the void BEFORE we measure. Sample the centroid and boundary vertices BEFORE applying `tileset.clippingPolygons` (or cache the pre-clip picks and re-use them), and the only picks that directly measure the answer come back — no footprint mask, no second data source, no plane fit.\n\nBEFORE ANY CODE CHANGES, run the author's own falsifier, because one log line carries the whole diagnosis: confirm the console actually printed `arm=slope` with n=58, and log the 58 picks WITH (lon,lat). If it printed `plateau` or `roof-gap`, the mechanism above is wrong and the seat came from the p10, which sends the investigation to why the ring's low decile is 14 m above the street instead of to the classifier.",
    "Keep the diagnosis; replace the fix's arms 1, 2 and 4. Corrected plan, ordered by what is actually shippable:\n\nSTEP 1 (unchanged, do it first). Prereq (0): make safeSampleTileHeights return {lon,lat,h}[]. Cheaper than the author thinks -- both extract lambdas already hold a Cesium.Cartographic. Two production callers plus one test.\n\nSTEP 2 -- THE ARM THAT NEEDS NO NEW DATA, and the one the fix missed. The picks are drawn from THREE KNOWN RADII (vertices x1.7, ext+25, ext+55). Once (lon,lat) survives, tag each pick with its radius and its bearing. A HILLSIDE produces a height that is a monotone function of BEARING at every radius, coherent across all three (the uphill sector is high at r=ext+25 AND at r=ext+55). ROOFS produce a height that varies with RADIUS at fixed bearing (a pick that crosses a street then a building) and is bearing-incoherent between the two compass rings. That is a positional discriminator computed entirely from data the sampler already has -- no OSM, no terrain, no gate change. It is the smallest change that makes 'slope' a positively-established finding instead of a fall-through, which is the actual defect.\n\nSTEP 3 -- the honest refusal, which the fix omits entirely. When the arms disagree, or when survivors fall below OUTLIER_ROBUST_MIN_SAMPLES=12, the answer is UNRESOLVED, not a guess. That path already exists and is already correct: decideGroundAnchorAction holds the building HIDDEN and retries, and the module's own header argues for it ('Refusing to place is cheap; placing wrongly is not'). Every arm the fix proposes silently produces a plausible number when its data is absent; none refuses.\n\nSTEP 4 -- if the OSM mask is still wanted, source it correctly and gate it. Read the footprints from contextBuildings.ts's prefetched bbox cache (:5114 warms it even on the photoreal path), NOT from contextBuildingPlacements[], which is empty there. Apply it ONLY as a corroborator of step 2, never as the primary, and abandon it -- loudly -- if fewer than 12 picks survive.\n\nDROP arm (2) as written. It cannot run without amending the ADR-0268 §156 photoreal gate, and in its absent-data state it silently reproduces the pre-L-12919 p10 rule, i.e. it re-ships the Sète bug wearing a citation.\n\nDROP arm (4) as written. A relative ceiling whose margin is zero on a linear slope, and negative on a convex one, will re-bury Sète. If a ceiling is wanted at all it must be validated against the Sète corpus BEFORE the Barcelona one, since Sète is the case it can break and Barcelona is the case it is tuned on.\n\nREPHRASE item (6) to 'requalify', never 'delete'.\n\nAND FIX THE DIAGNOSTIC (not in the claim): make CesiumViewport.ts:9380 classify the same `credible` set that globeGroundAnchor.ts:327 seats from, so the console cannot print an arm the seat did not take. Otherwise the founder-console corpus the fix wants to build in item (5) is captured from the wrong population.\n\nFINALLY -- the diagnosis's one unverified premise is still unverified, and it is one command away. The claim's own falsifier (is the site flat?) has not been run. Before ANY of the above, log the 58 picks with (lon,lat) and check the site's lat/lon against a contour map. If the ground really is a 20-40% slope, the median seat is correct and the float has a different cause -- and every step above is wasted work."

    "rootCause": "Both requirements reduce to ONE mechanism: **a space envelope's vertical extent is SEEDED from the level at create time and then never re-derived, and nothing anywhere classifies a face as \"horizontal\" for the purpose of gating a gesture.** (b) `SpaceEnvelope.baseOffset`/`height` are independent stored fields (`packages/schemas/src/elements/SpaceEnvelope.ts:211,218`). The ONLY place the level is consulted is `envelopeAuthoringPlan.ts:658-659` (`baseOffset: level.elevation`, `height: heightM` from `resolveStoreyHeight`, which prefers `level.height`). After that the coupling is gone: `packages/command-registry/` contains ZERO references to `SpaceEnvelope` (grep), so `SetLevelHeightCommand` — the real level-height authority — cannot and does not touch envelopes; and `CesiumViewport.renderSpaceEnvelopes` reads `rec.height`/`rec.baseOffset` off the record, never the level. So editing a storey height today leaves every envelope on that storey silently stale. (a) The authority is `SetLevelHeightCommand` (`packages/command-registry/src/levels/SetLevelHeightCommand.ts:150`, ADR-0345) — δ-cascade of every level above, re-seat of furniture/plumbing/lighting, ONE undo. It is reachable ONLY through the legacy `commandManager` (`LevelManagerPanel.ts:196`, `PropertyPanel.ts:1277`); there is NO bus verb for it — `level.update` bridges to `UpdateLevelCommand` (`initBusHandlers.ts:2355-2358`), which by `SetLevelHeightCommand`'s own header \"writes the number and fires NOTHING\". The Envelope card dispatches on `runtime.bus`, so the missing seam for requirement 1 is a bus→legacy bridge, not a new store. Crucially, both sides already read the SAME level authority: the card's `readLevels()` is `window.bimManager.getLevels()` (`parcelLawEnvelopeAuthoring.ts:418`), which is exactly what `SetLevelHeightCommand` writes via `bimManager.updateLevel`. (c) The horizontal/vertical distinction ALREADY EXISTS as a closed type — `SpaceEnvelopeFaceRef = {kind:'side',edgeIndex} | {kind:'top'} | {kind:'bottom'}` — and `spaceEnvelopeFaceAxis` gives top `+Y`, bottom `−Y`, side the ring's outward normal with `y:0` (`SpaceEnvelopeFaceDrag.ts:74-78`). What is missing is any GATE on it: `spaceEnvelopeFaces()` returns all n+2 including both caps (`SpaceEnvelopeGeometry.ts:145-151`), so `spaceEnvelopeFaceHandles` draws arrows on the caps; both live `pickFace` implementations return caps; `planSpaceEnvelopeFaceMove` explicitly implements cap moves (`SpaceEnvelopeFaceMove.ts:215-241`); and a repo-wide grep for `allowedFaces|lockVertical|faceFilter|canDragFace|isFaceDraggable` finds nothing.     "proposedFix": "TWO independent change sets. Ship requirement 2 first — it is small, self-contained, and does not depend on requirement 1.\n\n=== REQUIREMENT 2 · pin the horizontal faces (≈1 day, 4 files, no new subsystem) ===\nThe rule is a GESTURE policy keyed on the existing `SpaceEnvelopeFaceRef.kind`. It must land in TWO places or the affordance lies.\n\n1. `packages/geometry-space-envelope/src/SpaceEnvelopeFaceGizmo.ts:147` — add `readonly omitCapFaces?: boolean` to `SpaceEnvelopeFaceHandleOptions` and skip `top`/`bottom` in the `spaceEnvelopeFaceHandles` loop (:273). One rule in the pure package; the alternative (filtering at each call site) is two copies that can drift, which the gizmo header explicitly forbids.\n2. `apps/editor/src/engine/SpaceEnvelopeFaceGizmoBuilder.ts:229` and `apps/editor/src/ui/site/siteEnvelopeDrawCesium.ts:892` — pass `{ omitCapFaces: true }`. One line each. No arrow is drawn on a cap on either surface.\n3. `apps/editor/src/engine/spaceEnvelopeDragSurface.ts:602` — in `onPointerDown`, immediately after the existing `maximumBuildable` role refusal, add:\n   `if (face.kind !== 'side') { deps.onRefusal?.('The top and bottom of an envelope are pinned to its storey — they are set by the level height, not by dragging. Change the storey height in the Envelope card and this face follows. Drag a vertical face to change the outline.'); return; }`\n   This is the correct seam because it is AFTER the pick (so hover still lights the cap and the user learns why) and BEFORE `active` is set (so no drag starts). It is one place, not three: the same file's `pick()` wrapper documents that drag-start, hover and double-click are the only three pick consumers.\n   ⛔ Do NOT put the gate in `spaceEnvelopeFaceAxis`, `pickSpaceEnvelopeFace` or `planSpaceEnvelopeFaceMove` — `adaptRoomToMovedLevel` (SpaceEnvelopeContext.ts:346,357) drives cap moves through the planner to make rooms follow a storey, and blocking there breaks room adaptation.\n4. Tests: extend `packages/geometry-space-envelope/__tests__/spaceEnvelopeFaceGizmo.test.ts` (no cap handles under the option) and `apps/editor/src/ui/site/__tests__/siteEnvelopeFaceDragCesium.spec.ts` (a cap pointer-down raises the refusal and dispatches nothing).\n\n=== REQUIREMENT 1 · per-level height in the Envelope card, fed back to the author ===\nThe fact has ONE owner already — `level.height`, written by `SetLevelHeightCommand`. Do not mint a second. Three seams:\n\nA · THE WRITE PATH (the missing bridge).\n - `packages/command-bus/src/commands.ts:975` — declare `'level.setHeight': { levelId: string; height: number }` beside `level.add`.\n - `apps/editor/src/engine/initBusHandlers.ts:2353` — add the bridge in the \"level bridges\" block using **`_cmExecOrRefuse`** (:664), NOT `_cmExec`: SetLevelHeightCommand's `canExecute` refuses on level-crossing with both numbers, and `_cmExec` discards that verdict. The panel must be able to show the refusal.\n\nB · THE UI.\n - `apps/editor/src/ui/analysis/parcelLawEnvelopeAuthoring.ts` — the card headed \"Create the envelope\" (:746). It already reads `readLevelCandidates(deps.readLevels())` (:929) and already renders a per-storey row list via `storeyRows()` (:982) carrying *Edit perimeter* / *Drag face*. Add to each row a numeric height input seeded from `level.height`, and a total-height readout above the list (Σ of `resolveStoreyHeight(l, ordinance).heightM` — reuse the existing ladder, do not re-derive, and keep its `heightSource` so an assumed 3 m is still labelled as assumed). On commit dispatch `level.setHeight` through `deps.runtime()?.bus`, the same channel `addLevelsBtn` already uses (:1455-1476), awaited one at a time for the same documented reason.\n\nC · THE CONSEQUENCE (what makes the envelopes adapt).\n Use the §ENVELOPE-DRAG-CONSEQUENCE pattern the drag already established (`spaceEnvelopeDragSurface.ts:~470`: \"a consequence that wants to move a wall dispatches its OWN command from its OWN handler, and that is a second undo entry by design\"). Do NOT add a `@pryzm/geometry-space-envelope` dependency to `packages/command-registry` — that is a new cross-layer edge for a UI-level consequence.\n - New file, e.g. `apps/editor/src/engine/initSpaceEnvelopeLevelFollow.ts`, subscribing to `bim-level-updated` (already emitted by SetLevelHeightCommand execute step 4, and already known to `geometryMutationEvents.ts:103`). For every `role:'level'` envelope whose `levelId` is in the affected set, re-seat `baseOffset := level.elevation` and `height := level.height` via `spaceEnvelope.setParameter`.\n - **Required handler change:** `plugins/space-envelope/src/handlers/MutateSpaceEnvelope.ts:411` — for `role:'level'`, `setParameter` must use `containmentOutcomeFor` (adapt the rooms) rather than `containmentRefusalFor` (refuse), matching what `setFootprint` (:307,:323) and `moveFace` already do. Otherwise shrinking a storey refuses where the identical top-face drag succeeds.\n - **Undo cost, stated honestly:** SetLevelHeightCommand is one entry on the legacy stack; N envelope re-seats are N entries on the bus stack. If one Ctrl+Z is required, add a `spaceEnvelope.batch.setParameter` verb alongside the existing `spaceEnvelope.batch.create` (CreateSpaceEnvelopeBatch.ts:118) — that is the only batch verb today. Decide this explicitly; do not let it emerge.\n\nD · WHAT NOT TO DO.\n - Do not give the Envelope card its own height field per envelope — the card's shipped prose (:646) already tells the user storey height comes from the project's storeys.\n - Do not route level height through `level.update`; it reaches `UpdateLevelCommand`, which writes the number and cascades nothing.\n - Note the `baseOffset` convention before touching it: `envelopeAuthoringPlan.ts:197` records that four consumers treat `baseOffset` as ABSOLUTE above the project datum, contradicting C114's literal \"level-relative\" wording. So 'a room's top is pinned to its level's top' is NOT the current semantic, and making it so is a separate decision about whether a room may be shorter than its storey (mezzanines, dropped ceilings).      "betterHypothesis": "Keep the mechanism, fix four factual errors, and weigh two rivals the author never named.\n\nSHARPENED ROOT CAUSE (survives adversarial reading):\n(1) A `role:'level'` space envelope stores a DUPLICATE of two facts the level already owns (`height`, and a base that is meant to be the storey seat), written once at create by TWO independent producers with CONTRADICTORY datum conventions — `envelopeAuthoringPlan.ts:658` seats at `level.elevation`, `adoptProposalAsEnvelope.ts:330` seats at `0` — and read back verbatim by every rasteriser. No production code re-derives, re-seats or even writes those fields afterwards by any route (`spaceEnvelope.setParameter` has ZERO production dispatch sites in the whole repo). The duplication, not the missing subscriber, is the defect; the missing subscriber is only the most visible symptom.\n(2) Independently, the horizontal/vertical distinction exists as a closed discriminant and is honoured by geometry, picking, handles and the planner, but NO layer treats it as a GESTURE policy — so a cap drag is fully wired on both surfaces.\n\nRIVAL 1 THE AUTHOR DID NOT CONSIDER — derive, don't cache. Once requirement 2 pins the caps for `role:'level'`, that envelope's `height`/`baseOffset` become write-only-by-the-level, i.e. DERIVED. Storing them and repairing them from a `bim-level-updated` subscriber is a cache with a refresher: it is wrong for the whole window between the level write and the repair, and it diverges permanently on every path that bypasses the event — persistence load of a project saved before the fix, undo of a level edit, IFC/Revit import, any future level writer. That is a second answer to \"how tall is this storey\" (C84 EI-9), which is the rule the plan invokes against a per-envelope height field and then breaks with its own subscriber. Resolving `height`/`baseOffset` for `role:'level'` at read time from `levelId` removes the class instead of scheduling a repair for it. It is more work (the mesh builder, the Cesium rasteriser, the plan-symbol builder and `roomEnvelopePlan` all read the record), but it is the answer that cannot go stale — and the plan should at minimum state why it is rejected.\n\nRIVAL 2 THE AUTHOR DID NOT CONSIDER — route the cap drag instead of refusing it. The plan's requirement 2 removes an affordance. The alternative that satisfies the same ownership rule is to make a cap drag on a `role:'level'` envelope DISPATCH the storey-height change (through the very `level.setHeight` bridge seam A already builds) rather than raise a refusal. The user gets \"the level height owns this, and here is the handle that sets it\" instead of \"this handle is gone.\" Same single owner, no dead arrow, and it reuses seam A rather than needing an unrelated fourth file. Whether the founder wants the gesture removed or redirected is a question for him — the plan should not settle it silently on the removal side.\n\nREQUIRED CORRECTIONS TO THE PLAN AS WRITTEN:\nA. Before ANY re-seat subscriber, reconcile the two datum producers. `adoptProposalAsEnvelope.ts:330` writes `baseOffset: 0`; a subscriber writing `baseOffset := level.elevation` will teleport every envelope that path created. Pick one convention, migrate both producers, and only then add the consequence — or scope the subscriber to `height` alone and leave `baseOffset` to L-13147.\nB. Drop the \"level.update fires NOTHING / would be a silent no-op\" reasoning. `UpdateLevelCommand:90` already emits `bim-level-updated`. Seam A is needed for the δ-cascade of the levels ABOVE, and for the refusal `_cmExecOrRefuse` surfaces — not for the envelope, which seam C picks up either way. Justify the bridge on the cascade, or the reviewer will find the emit and distrust the rest.\nC. Scope the cap gate by role: `if (record.role === 'level' && face.kind !== 'side')`. Room caps drag today and the planner models them; the plan's unconditional refusal is a regression with a sentence that is untrue for rooms.\nD. Do not rely on `heightSource` for honesty. `BimKernel.addLevel` coerces a missing height to 3.0, so `resolveStoreyHeight` always reports `'level-record'` for a bimManager level and `'assumed-3m'` is unreachable in production. Either make the coercion visible (a nullable `height` on the Level record, or a flag) or drop the honesty label rather than print a false one.\nE. Settle the undo question before writing code, as the plan itself says: SetLevelHeightCommand is one legacy entry and N envelope re-seats are N bus entries. Note that `roomEnvelopePlan` copies the level envelope's `baseOffset`/`height` into every room, so the re-seat fan-out is levels x rooms, not levels."

     "betterHypothesis": "The root cause needs no revision. The FIX does — four defects, ranked. Requirement 2's gizmo+gate half is sound and shippable roughly as written (the four files and the `onPointerDown` seam are all correct, `onRefusal` reaches a toast on the 3-D Site, and `setActiveFace` degrades safely when a cap has no node). Requirement 1's seam C is where this would ship a confidently wrong answer.\n\n1 · THE INVARIANT THE CODE IS PROTECTING, AND THE FIX BREACHES IT BY OMISSION. `containmentGate.ts` states it in its own header: \"That function is called by SIX verbs, and only some of them WRITE the rooms they would adapt. Loosening it centrally would make `setParameter`, `move` and `batch.create` stop refusing a level that strands a room while still writing only the level — leaving the room outside its storey with nothing anywhere saying so, which is strictly worse than the refusal it replaced. So the strict gate keeps its callers, and a verb that is prepared to WRITE the adaptation opts into this one.\" The rule is not \"use containmentOutcomeFor\"; it is \"you may use it IFF your execute writes the adapted rooms.\" `setFootprint.execute` (:320-347) does exactly that — `withMetrics` per adapted room, all in ONE `produceCommand`. The fix says only \"must use `containmentOutcomeFor` … rather than `containmentRefusalFor`\" and never states the write obligation. Implemented literally, it produces the documented catastrophic outcome verbatim. Restate seam C as: setParameter must adopt BOTH halves of setFootprint's shape — gate and commit ask the same question (C84 EI-9.2), and the adapted rooms land in the same patch pair.\n\n2 · `adaptRoomToMovedLevel` SHRINKS ROOMS; IT DOES NOT TRANSLATE THEM. This is the sharpest hazard and neither the claim nor the fix mentions it. SetLevelHeightCommand moves every level ABOVE the edited one by δ (elevation only — each keeps its own height). Seam C re-seats those envelopes' `baseOffset := level.elevation`, so the level prism translates up by δ with its height unchanged. Now run SpaceEnvelopeContext.ts:355-364 on a room inside it: `below = level.baseY − room.baseY = δ > 0` fires a `{kind:'bottom'}` move of −δ, which raises the room's FLOOR and leaves its ceiling where it was; `above = room.topY − level.topY` is now negative, so nothing raises the top. Net: every room on every storey above the edit gets δ shorter and sits wrong inside its storey — silently, in the same undo entry, with the containment check reporting `contained`. A rigid translation of a level demands a rigid translation of its rooms, and the adapter in this package cannot express one. Either add a translate path or have seam C move room `baseOffset` itself, and pin it with a test that asserts room HEIGHT is invariant under a pure level translation.\n\n3 · `bim-level-updated` IS THE WRONG TRIGGER, THREE WAYS. (a) It carries `{ id: levelId }` — the EDITED level only. SetLevelHeightCommand:328 names none of the levels it translated, so the \"affected set\" seam C wants to iterate does not exist in the payload; the subscriber must re-derive from the whole level list. (b) `UpdateLevelCommand:90` emits the same event for ANY level edit — name, colour, visibility — so a rename would re-dispatch N envelope writes unless seam C delta-guards on the actual numbers. (c) DECISIVE: it is emitted on UNDO (SetLevelHeightCommand:404, UpdateLevelCommand:115). Undoing a height edit would wake the subscriber and push N NEW FORWARD `spaceEnvelope.setParameter` entries onto the bus stack — the stack grows during an undo. The §ENVELOPE-DRAG-CONSEQUENCE precedent the fix leans on does NOT transfer: that consequence runs on the pointer-UP handler of a gesture, and nothing re-fires a pointer-up on undo. Trigger seam C from the `level.setHeight` bridge itself (where you know both the δ and the moved set, and where undo does not re-enter), not from the DOM event.\n\n4 · REQUIREMENT 2 ORPHANS LIVE MACHINERY AND SILENTLY CLOSES AN OPEN ROW. `spaceEnvelopeWallFollowPlan.ts` carries a whole cap-move branch — `ring-unchanged` (logged, deliberately not toasted, :630/:938) plus a separately TOASTED bottom-drag refusal (:181, :318-322) — and L-13118 is OPEN in ISSUE-LOG with a named, costed exit (\"what is missing is the EVENT FIELD, not the verb: add `height`/`baseOffset` to `SpaceEnvelopeFaceMoveCommitted`\"). Forbidding cap drags makes all of it unreachable. That may be precisely the founder's intent, but the fix must retire L-13118 in the same commit and say why, or a later lane builds the height leg for a gesture that can no longer fire — the [[authored-but-unwired]] shape, minted deliberately.\n\nTwo smaller notes. (i) On Cesium, `pickNearestSpaceEnvelopeFace` returns `top` for any ray hitting the roof, which from a near-overhead camera is the largest target on the envelope; every such click would now raise a warning toast, and because `onDoubleClick` runs IN ADDITION to `onPointerDown`, a double-click to open the outline editor raises the cap refusal twice. Prefer log-only, or once per gesture. (ii) The `baseOffset` datum is a genuine live contradiction — `packages/schemas/src/elements/SpaceEnvelope.ts:211` says \"above the owning level's datum\" while `envelopeAuthoringPlan.ts:182-200` documents four rasterisers treating it as absolute above the project datum, filed as L-13147. Seam C writing `baseOffset := level.elevation` follows the code, which is right; do not let anyone \"fix\" the prose half and the code half in different commits."

    "defectId": "massing-blocked-by-envelope-refusal",
    "rootCauseFound": true,
    "rootCause": "The legal determination and the massing affordance are coupled in ONE function. refreshEnvelopePanel (GISAreaLayout.ts) has three arms - absence, refusal, full determination - and the massing-options fold is rendered and wired in the FULL-DETERMINATION ARM ONLY (:5846 render, :5863 wire). The founder's Barcelona clau-22@ parcel produces legallyGrounded:true (esBarcelonaZoneClassification.ts:920), which siteDispatch.ts:7833 maps to status 'not-applicable' with a refusal, so refreshEnvelopePanel takes the refusal arm at :5179 and returns at :5362 - before the fold. That fold is the ONLY consumer of buildAuthoredMassingOptionHtml (one call site, envelopeCardSections.ts:582), i.e. the \"Create it myself\" button whose own doc reads \"Present on every arm - the tool is never gated\" (massingAuthoredOptionSection.ts:43). An explicitly un-gated authoring route therefore sits inside a gated container. On that same arm two more affordances are withheld: the study-height entry (:5241, (isAbsent || isGap) ? ... : '' - both false for a legally-grounded refusal) and the manual-zone button. Feed a drawn ring in and PRYZM prints \"the permitted buildable footprint here is N m²\" on the exact parcel where the ordinance said no envelope applies. (3) REPLACE — on the non-determination arms render the AUTHORED card and SUPPRESS the Generate button (a fold state that carries no generator). The coverage and shape families are DEFINED as fractions of a permitted footprint and say so in ~15 sentences across two modules; routing a drawn ring through them is authored geometry borrowing a solved tier (C58 §1.19 cl.3). If the founder wants generated shapes inside his own perimeter, that is a separate named lane that must (a) thread `ringBasis` into every sentence builder and rename/null the `…OfPermitted` fields, (b) give `TargetFootprintProposal` a basis field so the staleness gate keys on the drawn-ring area instead of a permitted area that is 0, and (c) stop the refusal arm's :5122 `resolveLiveTargetFootprintProposal(null)` from destroying the proposal on every repaint — use the non-destructive peek the absence arm already has for `cannotJudgeStaleness`, and extend `wireTargetAreaAdopt` to that arm, or pick is a dead end.     "defectId": "house-layout-modal-on-bim-entry",
    "rootCauseFound": true,
    "rootCause": "The \"Design your house — live\" screen is `buildHouseModalHtml` (`apps/editor/src/ui/house-layout/houseModalHtml.ts:588`, 3-pane: plan LEFT · graph CENTER · sliders/tools RIGHT), mounted as a transient overlay by `HouseLayoutModal.show` via a direct `document.body.appendChild(overlay)` (`HouseLayoutModal.ts:613`) — not through PanelManager. It is shown from exactly one place in the codebase: `HouseLayoutController.request` → `this.modal.show(...)` (`HouseLayoutController.ts:333`). That is reached on entry to the BIM/PRYZM views by two production call sites, both of which leave `HouseFromBoundaryOptions.autoBuild` at its default `false` (the modal path): (1) the Parcel Law tab's question 7 \"Take me into BIM.\" Create-house button — `parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:492` calls `deps.buildHouse(...)`, whose production impl at `parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:215` is `generateHouseFromBoundary(rt, storeyCount, {...})` with `autoBuild` deliberately NOT passed; and (2) `OnboardingStepController.generateHouse()` at `:4123`, which awaits `generateHouseFromBoundary(...)` and then hands the pane to `pryzmShowSiteResultView('2D')` at `:4085` — and because `request()` resolves the instant the chooser opens (`HouseLayoutController.ts:109`), the modal is literally on screen as the BIM dual pane comes up. I swept every reachable upstream: `generateHouseFromBoundary` has exactly 4 non-test callers (`parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:215`, `OnboardingStepController.ts:4123`, `generationChatSeam.ts:649` — which passes `autoBuild:true` → `buildDirect`, no modal — and `houseLayoutTrigger.ts:34`, the console command installed at `AIAreaLayout.ts:289`); `generateHouseInExistingShell` only from the same console trigger; no AI-panel leaf, no `pryzmGenerateHouse` caller anywhere in app code, no auto-`.click()` of `CREATE_HOUSE_BTN_TESTID` (the only programmatic clicks are `parcelLawChat.ts`, which drives *other* testids from a typed user utterance), and nothing on a view-activation path. The gates before `modal.show` are exactly the four named (active level · shell · C58 `checkMaxHeightGate` · `variants.length > 0`) — no flag. `houseOrchestrator.ts:19` does import `generateDeterministicLayouts` from the apartment TGL, and `enumerate.ts:296` does say the pool \"is NEVER emptied\". The three-line fall-through is verbatim at `parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:334-336`, and I confirmed the mechanics of removing it: `planBuildFromDesign` reaches `ok` with zero rooms (the `every-room-refused` guard at :1067 is `rooms.length > 0 && builtRooms.length === 0`), and `buildBuildFromDesignSection` renders an ENABLED button on any `ok` outcome. I also killed the best \"data-shape\" rival: `resolveStore` reads `rt.stores.spaceEnvelope`, the same store `roomProgrammePanel` writes via `spaceEnvelope.batch.create`, and `readDesignEnvelopes` filters on the same `role` field `roomEnvelopePlan.ts:238` writes — so the fourth arm is not silently blind.\n\n⛔ TWO LOAD-BEARING CLAIMS DO NOT SURVIVE, AND BOTH ARE ABOUT THE FIX, NOT THE MECHANISM.\n\n(1) \"REMOVING THE FALL-THROUGH COSTS NO CAPABILITY / IS STRICTLY SOUNDER\" IS FALSE, AND THE CODE SAYS SO IN ITS OWN VOICE. `BUILD_FROM_DESIGN_WILL_NOT_CREATE` (`buildFromDesignPlan.ts:397-412`) explicitly excludes **a ROOF**, **STAIRS and slab voids**, **floor finishes**, and **new project levels** (\"every storey your plates name already exists\"). On the exact case the fix targets — one level envelope, no rooms — question 7 goes from *an N-storey house with rooms, a stair, a stairwell void and a roof* to *a ring of shell walls plus one slab*. Worse, that list's own text tells the user to **\"Ask for one on the house arm, where the form is an input you give\"** — the arm the fix would make unreachable from any UI, leaving the panel pointing at a door it just bricked. And the fourth arm's head copy is hard-coded to *\"You have drawn a level envelope and room envelopes, so PRYZM builds THOSE\"* with a *\"Your 0 rooms (0.00 m²)\"* list beneath it (`createHouseSection.ts:163-166, 244-247`) — a sentence that is simply untrue in the zero-rooms case the fix routes there. Shipping the three-line deletion without rewriting that arm's copy is the §CONTEXT-DATA-HONESTY class this repo gates on. The claim's own citation of `buildFromDesignPlan.ts:678` also over-reads it: the author said the fall-through is \"not something this lane should change silently\", which is a caution about ownership, not an invitation.\n\n(2) THE ATTRIBUTION TO THIS SURFACE IS UNDER-DETERMINED, AND THE TIMING ARGUES AGAINST IT. `armLandInBimOnNextHouse` fires on `house.layout-executed` — emitted AFTER the executor lands, not on `modal.show` (its header says so and `landInBimAfterCreateHouse.ts:87-120` proves it), and the create-house click branch (`parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:489`) arms it BEFORE `buildHouse` and never navigates itself. So on the Parcel Law path the modal opens **over the site/analysis view** and BIM entry happens only after the user picks. On the onboarding path, `generateHouse()` (`:4123`) resolves the instant the chooser opens and control falls straight to `showResult('2D')` (`:4085`) — the modal and the BIM dual pane come up **together**, which is the literal reading of the defect slug \"on-bim-entry\". The counter-evidence is real and I weighed it: the L-13080 precedent recorded in `buildFromDesignPlan.ts:1-8` is the founder saying this after clicking \"Take me into BIM\", so the Parcel Law reading has a documented prior. Both fit; the claim discloses the ambiguity honestly and then fixes only one. Do not ship the Parcel Law edit and report the defect closed.\n\nI am returning refuted=false because the causal chain is right and I exhausted the search for a different mount. But the fix as written is not the free swap it claims to be.",

     "betterHypothesis": "Not a different mount — there isn't one — but a different DEFECT BOUNDARY and a different fix. Rival: the modal appearing at question 7 with no rooms is not a routing accident, it is L-13080's deliberately preserved offer (`buildFromDesignPlan.ts:678`), and what the founder is actually hitting is the thing behind it — the never-refusing TGL engine (`enumerate.ts:296`) fed a programme sized to the whole buildable plate, which is already logged as L-13023 / L-13034, both OPEN and both marked FOUNDER DECISION. Under that reading, deleting `parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:334-336` removes a symptom on one of two surfaces while silently costing the roof, the stair, the stairwell void and multi-storey level minting, and leaving the panel asserting \"you have drawn … room envelopes\" over an empty room list. Better fix: on `design.ok && plan.rooms.length === 0`, keep BOTH arms on the surface — render the build-from-design arm as the primary (with rewritten zero-rooms copy: \"no rooms declared, so this builds the shell and the plate only\") plus the pointer to question 4 AND the explicit \"Place envelopes in 3D\" step, and keep the generator as a clearly-labelled secondary (\"generate a layout instead — this proposes rooms, a stair and a roof PRYZM chose\"). That answers \"it should work for walls/slabs, minimum\" without amputating the only path to a roof. Separately, and before any of it: settle which call site produced the report. The two paths are observably different — Parcel Law puts the modal over the SITE view and enters BIM only after the pick (`landInBimAfterCreateHouse` fires on `house.layout-executed`), while onboarding (`OnboardingStepController:4123` → `:4085`) puts the modal and the BIM dual pane on screen together, which is what \"on BIM entry\" literally describes. Ask the founder whether he came from the onboarding wizard or the Parcel Law tab; the one-line Parcel Law edit is a no-op for the first."

     "refuted": true,
     "reason": "The DIAGNOSIS is accurate — I confirmed every structural claim (single modal, single show site at HouseLayoutController.ts:333, request() resolving at modal-open, three prod callers with two on the modal path, and the three-line fall-through at parcelLawCreateHouse.ts:334-336 being the only thing routing Q7's button to the generator). The FIX is refuted on three counts.\n\n(1) WRONG SURFACE FOR THE REPORTED SYMPTOM. The Parcel Law tab is not a BIM surface: SiteSurface.ts:1-3 (\"SITE workspace mode's right-hand half\", ADR-0343 §D.1, a top-level MODE left of Author) and :394 is the one live mountParcelLawTab call in the shell. On that path the chooser opens over SITE, and BIM entry happens only AFTER the pick — HouseLayoutModal.ts:577 does `this.dismiss(); cb.onSelect(idx)`, and landInBimAfterCreateHouse fires on `house.layout-executed`, which its own header says is deliberately NOT the pipeline promise (\"Navigating on that promise would yank the camera out from under the chooser\"). The ONLY path where the modal is on screen AS the BIM dual pane comes up is OnboardingStepController.generateHouse() (:4123, awaits the promise that resolves at modal-open) followed by showResult('2D') (:4085) — which the proposal explicitly declines to change. For a defect named \"modal on BIM entry\", the proposed edit is most likely a no-op on the screen the founder actually saw. The author's own falsification test cannot detect this, because it only exercises the Parcel Law click.\n\n(2) \"COSTS NO CAPABILITY\" IS FALSE, and this is the load-bearing error. HOUSE_WILL_CREATE (createHousePlan.ts:126-135) = storey levels AND a dedicated roof level, shell walls, generated partitions, rooms, floor slabs, a ROOF, STAIRS between storeys, floor finishes and ceilings — and the executor really does mint levels (HouseLayoutExecutor.ts:447-459, AddLevelCommand) and build a roof (:497). BUILD_FROM_DESIGN_WILL_NOT_CREATE (buildFromDesignPlan.ts:397-419) explicitly refuses, by name: a generated layout, NEW PROJECT LEVELS, a ROOF (\"no space envelope carries a roof form… Ask for one on the house arm\"), FLOOR FINISHES, STAIRS (\"and no void is punched through the slabs\"), doors or windows, columns/beams, and room records. So on a zero-room plate — precisely the branch being redirected — the Q7 button goes from producing a complete multi-storey house to producing shell walls plus one slab per storey. That is a large, user-visible capability loss on the exact population this branch serves, not \"strictly sounder at no cost\".\n\n(3) IT BREAKS A PROTECTED INVARIANT, TWO NAMED SPECS, AND ITS OWN COPY. The invariant is stated as binding in createHouseSection.ts's header: STR §25.0 \"I want pryzm to guide this process without building the house in one click\" is given as the reason this button leaves autoBuild at false; the build-from-design arm builds immediately on click with no chooser, so the redirect converts Q7 into the one-click builder §25.0 forbids (defensible for a bare shell, but it is a product reversal, not a neutral routing change — and the L-13250 comment the proposal cites as licence actually says the opposite: \"not something this lane should change silently\"). Two specs assert the current behaviour verbatim and would fail: parcelLawCreateHouse.spec.ts:505 \"WITH NO ROOMS the generator arm is kept, word for word, advisory included\" and :769 \"WITHOUT a room drawn, the SAME slot still carries the generator — unchanged\" (which also asserts `q(slot, BUILD_FROM_DESIGN_TESTID)` is null). And the zero-room build-from-design arm renders false copy the proposal does not fix: the head says \"You have drawn a level envelope AND ROOM ENVELOPES, so PRYZM builds THOSE\" (createHouseSection.ts head of buildBuildFromDesignSection); the rooms block renders \"Your 0 rooms (0 m²)\" over an empty <ul>; and willCreate prints \"0 ceilings — one over each room you drew, at its storey's finished clear height (0 m on the lowest storey)\" from `ceilings[0]?.ceilingHeightM ?? 0` (buildFromDesignPlan.ts:1154-1156) — a fabricated 0 m, the §CONTEXT-DATA-HONESTY shape this repo already tracks.\n\nTwo smaller notes: the routing analysis is correct that a REFUSED design already shows the disabled build-from-design arm today (the fall-through is guarded by `design.ok`), so the fix's blast radius is exactly the design.ok && rooms===0 case; and the ordinals cited (rooms=4, bim=7, parcelLawQuestionGroup.ts:323/382) are right while the spec's \"Q6\" comments are stale.",

     "betterHypothesis": "The symptom named in the defect id — the modal sitting on top of the BIM views as they come up — is produced by the CONCURRENCY seam, not by the Parcel Law arm selection. `HouseLayoutController.request` resolves the moment `modal.show` is called (HouseLayoutController.ts:109), and `OnboardingStepController.generateHouse()` awaits that promise (:4123) and then immediately hands the pane to `pryzmShowSiteResultView('2D')` (:4085). That is the one code path in the repo where the chooser and BIM entry are simultaneous; the Parcel Law path deliberately defers BIM entry to `house.layout-executed` so the two never overlap.\n\nSo the first move is to establish WHICH surface the founder was on (his console will show `[onboarding-step] §FUTURE-TYPOLOGY (A.21.j) → HOUSE generator` for the wizard vs `[analysis][create-house]` for the tab), and the fix belongs at the onboarding seam: either wait for the real build (`house.layout-executed`) before calling showResult, or stop running the house generator on the `route === 'house'` branch and land the user in BIM with the parcel + level envelope. Either is a change to what \"Generate\" means for casa-unifamiliar and needs the founder decision the proposal already flags — which is an argument for doing that decision FIRST, not for shipping the Parcel Law edit as a consolation.\n\nIf the Parcel Law surface is genuinely in scope too, the sound version of the exclusion is not a straight arm swap. It would need, in the same lane: (a) both call sites, not one; (b) the roof/stairs/level-creation gap closed or explicitly accepted by the founder, since a zero-room plate currently yields a roofed multi-storey house and would yield a roofless shell; (c) the two specs at parcelLawCreateHouse.spec.ts:505 and :769 rewritten to the new intent; and (d) buildBuildFromDesignSection's head sentence, empty rooms list and the `(0 m on the lowest storey)` ceiling string made honest for rooms===0. A cheaper interim that keeps every capability and still removes the unsound screen is to leave the arm alone and make the generator path itself refuse rather than ship the least-bad layout (enumerate.ts:296 §TOPO-HARD-REJECT-ALL), which is the actual unsoundness the founder named."
    }

That is the one code path in the repo where the chooser and BIM entry are simultaneous; the Parcel Law path deliberately defers BIM entry to `house.layout-executed` so the two never overlap.\n\nSo the first move is to establish WHICH surface the founder was on (his console will show `[onboarding-step] §FUTURE-TYPOLOGY (A.21.j) → HOUSE generator` for the wizard vs `[analysis][create-house]` for the tab), and the fix belongs at the onboarding seam: either wait for the real build (`house.layout-executed`) before calling showResult, or stop running the house generator on the `route === 'house'` branch and land the user in BIM with the parcel + level envelope. Either is a change to what \"Generate\" means for casa-unifamiliar and needs the founder decision the proposal already flags — which is an argument for doing that decision FIRST, not for shipping the Parcel Law edit as a consolation.\n\nIf the Parcel Law surface is genuinely in scope too, the sound version of the exclusion is not a straight arm swap. It would need, in the same lane: (a) both call sites, not one; (b) the roof/stairs/level-creation gap closed or explicitly accepted by the founder, since a zero-room plate currently yields a roofed multi-storey house and would yield a roofless shell; (c) the two specs at parcelLawCreateHouse.spec.ts:505 and :769 rewritten to the new intent; and (d) buildBuildFromDesignSection's head sentence, empty rooms list and the `(0 m on the lowest storey)` ceiling string made honest for rooms===0. A cheaper interim that keeps every capability and still removes the unsound screen is to leave the arm alone and make the generator path itself refuse rather than ship the least-bad layout (enumerate.ts:296 §TOPO-HARD-REJECT-ALL), which is the actual unsoundness the founder named."
   ]
  }

    "proposedFix": "Three parts. Part 1 closes the brief's question; Part 3 removes the cause.\n\nPART 1 — make the refusal VERIFY, and rescue at the CURRENT site (never at `loadedAt`). And because it is the same (lat,lon) the correct load would use, the coalescer at :11828 joins an identical in-flight load and contextBuildings.ts's per-bbox cache answers a repeat with no network hop. Clear `reseatRescueFiredAt` in the project-switch teardown beside `this.contextLoadInFlight = null` (:18701).\n\nPART 2 — `currentContextSite()` (:9956) must read the LTP-ENU origin FIRST: `return this.readSiteLocation() ?? formaMassingOrigin`, inverting today's precedence. `formaMassingOrigin` is written only at :8209; the LTP origin is rebased inside `dispatchSiteLocation` BEFORE the event is emitted. Inverting makes both the refusal and the Part-1 rescue aim at the site the user is actually on during the window between the location dispatch and the next render — the window this defect lives in. Add one input, `frameMovedTo` (the LTP-ENU origin from `getCurrentSiteOrigin()`, already imported in CesiumViewport), and reject any candidate further than `RESEAT_ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M` (250 m) from it, BEFORE the radius test:\n- genuine site move (parcel reselect, new location before commit): `setLtpOriginIfSafe` rebased, LTP == requested, stale candidates are >250 m from it -> REJECTED -> the anchor is the LTP origin, i.e. the NEW parcel's frame origin (not the raw address either — this also closes the narrow-scope branch where `requested` wins);\n- post-commit address edit (the case L-13086 exists for): `setLtpOriginIfSafe` SKIPPED (siteDispatch.ts:1140), LTP still == the parcel, candidates co-located -> ADOPTED -> the parcel still beats the address. Roads, parks, water, landuse, rail, trees and street life are re-anchored only by `renderFormaMassing`, so on every path in G1/G2 those seven layers stay at the previous site even once the buildings move. Part 1 pulls trees and street life across; the five entity layers still need a decision.\n\nTESTS: extend `scopeAnchor.spec.ts` with a frame-moved case at 1143 m (must NOT adopt) beside the existing L-13086 post-commit case at the same distance (must adopt) — same distance, opposite verdicts, which is the whole point. Given the :11966 abort guard, that line will print the NEW parcel on the surviving load, at which point the anchor mechanism is not the fault.\n\nONE REAL, REACHABLE DEFECT DID SURFACE WHILE CHECKING, AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE FIX FROM PART 3: the codebase holds TWO incompatible definitions of \"the site's centre\". `resolveScopeAnchor` deliberately returns the parcel AREA CENTROID (scopeAnchor.ts's ⭐ ruling); `currentContextSite()` returns the parcel FIRST VERTEX (formaMassingOrigin ← getFormaOrigin ← LTP ← parcelFrameOrigin = ring[0]). On the ONE path where a location-changed load is NOT superseded by a renderFormaMassing — the geocode box (`siteGeocodeSearchBox.ts:162`) with a boundary already committed, which is precisely the L-13086 case the anchor was built for — the completed load stamps `contextBuildingsAt` / `contextFarTierState` at the CENTROID while `currentContextSite()` reports the FIRST VERTEX. On any parcel whose centroid is more than RESEAT_ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M (250 m) from its first vertex — the \"large rural or industrial parcel\" scopeAnchor.ts names — `reseatAnchorForCurrentSite` then refuses FOREVER, on every terrain settle, for trees, street life and the far tier, with no successor load anywhere. Parts 1 and 3 both resolve the same conflict in the other direction (Part 1 rescue-loads at the first vertex; Part 3 rejects the centroid whenever it is >250 m from the first vertex), reversing scopeAnchor.ts's headline ruling without saying so. If the anchor mechanism does turn out to be implicated after the probe, the minimal honest change is the one the diagnosis states but does not implement: make the refusal at :9979 VERIFY rather than assume — but against a per-layer in-flight handle, not the buildings-only `contextLoadInFlight`, and with a retryable (not one-shot-until-project-switch) rescue."

THE SUCCESSOR LOAD THE CLAIM SAYS DOES NOT EXIST IS ONE LINE BELOW THE LINE IT CITES. The claim cites `CesiumViewport.ts:8343` (`applySiteScopeClip(originLat, originLon, {skipSide:true})`, which it calls \":8320\") as proof the plate is re-cut at the new parcel. The very next statement, `:8344`, is `void this.loadContextBuildings(originLat, originLon);` — followed by roads/water/parks/landuse/rail/trees/street-life at `:8345-8351`. All are in ONE `try`, in the `else` of the photoreal branch, inside `if (!input._skipTerrainClamp)`. There is no reachable state that runs the plate cut and not the load. So \"plate re-cut at the NEW parcel while the buildings sit as a lobe 1.1 km away\" is unproducible, and the claim's own G1/G2/G3 (renderFormaMassing did not run) mutually exclude the plate half of its own picture.\n\n2. ON THE ONLY PATH THAT ACTUALLY SELECTS A DISTANT PARCEL, THE MIS-ANCHORED LOAD IS DEAD IN THE SAME TICK. `SiteBoundaryMap2D.ts:4059` calls `dispatchSiteLocation(parcelFrameOrigin(vertices))` — the NEW parcel's first vertex — and `siteDispatch.ts:1529` emits synchronously (`packages/event-bus/src/EventBus.ts:79`). The handler fires the mis-anchored load; then, still synchronously, `dispatchParcelBoundary` → `siteDispatch.ts:1804` emits `site.parcel-boundary-set` → `GISAreaLayout.ts:7054` → `renderFormaMassing` (no `await` anywhere between `:6593` and `:8360`) → `:8344` `loadContextBuildings(NEW)`. The coalescer does not join (different coords, `:11829`), and `:11899` aborts the first load's controller. That load then returns at `:11966` `if (signal.aborted …)` BEFORE `contextBuildingsAt = {lat,lon}` (`:11991`), before `applySiteScopeClip` (`:11998`), before the near placement, and before `contextFarTierState` (`:13352`). It seats nothing at all.\n\n3. THE 1143 m REFUSAL — THE CLAIM'S ONE PIECE OF FOUNDER EVIDENCE — HAS A STRICTLY SIMPLER CAUSE IT DID NOT RULE OUT. `contextFarTierState` is nulled only by `clearContextBuildings()` (`:13694`), which the new load reaches at `:12186`, i.e. AFTER the fetch + `ensureGroundBaseForContext` + near ground batch. `clampTerrainThenReplace` is fired at `:8285`, BEFORE the loads at `:8344`. On a relief city the clamp routinely settles inside that window, so `rebuildContextFarTierForBase` (`:13509`) reads the genuinely-previous site's stamp and refuses — exactly as its own doc predicts (\"the pending load rebuilds it correctly\"). That prints the founder's line on ANY site move >250 m (`RESEAT_ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M`, `:2185`) with no stale-anchor adoption anywhere. The claim reads a designed, benign line as the trace of its mechanism.\n\n4. RIVALS I BUILT AND KILLED (so they are not left as unexplored): a clip-centre race is impossible — `applySiteScopeClip` has no `await` before it writes `siteScopeClipAt` (`:2665-2790`), and `scopeClipperFor`'s cache key includes lat/lon to 8dp (`:2394`), so a moved centre can never read UNCHANGED. `plateAdmissionFor` builds its own clipper from the load anchor. Both agree with the load by construction, which is precisely why plate ≠ buildings cannot happen.\n\nNET: `resolveScopeAnchor`'s radius test is a real defect and Part 3 is a good fix, but it is not shown to cause this defect, and the described symptom is contradicted by `:8344`. Shipping this as THE root cause is the documented \"fixed it but he still reports it\" shape.",
    "THE CITATIONS ARE ACCURATE; THE CAUSAL CONCLUSION AND ALL THREE FIX PARTS ARE NOT. I verified essentially every quoted line (minor drift only: `applySiteScopeClip(originLat, originLon, {skipSide:true})` is at :8343, not :8320; the handler block is :5081-5103; `renderFormaMassing` spans 6593-8448 so :6979/:6991/:8209 are indeed inside it). What is wrong is what those lines do together.\n\n(1) THE CONSEQUENCE CHAIN IS SEVERED BY AN ABORT THE DIAGNOSIS NEVER EXAMINES — and severed on the exact path it leads with. `SiteBoundaryMap2D.commit()` calls `dispatchSiteLocation` (`:4060`) and then `dispatchParcelBoundary` in ONE synchronous span; `dispatchSiteLocation` emits `site.location-changed` synchronously (`siteDispatch.ts:1526-1529`), and the boundary dispatch's `site.parcel-boundary-set` reaches `liveUpdateFormaMassing` → the LOCAL `renderFormaMassing` (`GISAreaLayout.ts:5881`), which is a plain synchronous function. So `renderFormaMassing`'s own `applySiteScopeClip(originLat, originLon)` (:8343) and `loadContextBuildings(originLat, originLon)` (:8344) fire at the NEW origin in the same tick. `loadContextBuildingsUncoalesced` runs `this.contextBuildingsAbort?.abort()` BEFORE its first `await` (:11897), and the mis-anchored load's continuation is stopped by `if (signal.aborted || !this.viewer …) return;` at **:11966 — which precedes EVERY stamp the diagnosis depends on**: `contextBuildingsAt` (:11991), `void this.applySiteScopeClip(lat, lon)` (:11998) and the far-tier memo `contextFarTierState = { features: bounded, lat, lon }` (:13352). The mis-anchored load therefore stamps nothing and arms nothing on that path. \"Two owners of one centre\" cannot arise: the surviving buildings load and the plate are both at `originLat/originLon`. This is the author's OWN stated falsifier — `[CTX-DIAG] footprints fetched … for LAT … LON …` printing the NEW parcel on the load whose buildings are drawn — and the code says that is what will print.\n\n(2) THE \"DISTANT PARCEL\" FLOW CANNOT EVEN REACH THE CLAIMED WINDOW WITHOUT A CLEAR FIRST. `canCommitParcelBoundary` REFUSES a second commit while a boundary exists (`SiteBoundaryMap2D.ts:4024-4037`, \"REFUSE BEFORE MUTATING… Nothing was mutated\"). Selecting a distant new parcel requires `dispatchClearParcelBoundary` first — after which `setLtpOriginIfSafe` is no longer skipped and rebases anyway. G2's `map2d && !site3dPaned` window is the only place a mis-anchored load survives, and it self-heals on the view switch (`GISAreaLayout.ts:6616`, `:7938-7940` both call `renderFormaMassing` on switching to 3D); if Cesium is unmounted, `loadContextBuildingsUncoalesced` returns at `if (!viewer) return;` so there is no mis-anchored load either. The far-tier refusal at ~1143 m during the window between a site change and the new load's completion is EXPECTED, benign, and self-clearing — which is exactly what its premise at :9971-9974 says.\n\n(3) PART 3 SILENTLY REVERSES `scopeAnchor.ts`'s OWN HEADLINE RULING. Its ⭐ paragraph is \"THE ANCHOR IS THE PARCEL, NOT THE FRAME ORIGIN, WHICH ARE NOT THE SAME POINT… `parcelFrameOrigin` deliberately returns the ring's FIRST VERTEX… A slab centred on a corner of the parcel is off by the parcel's own first-vertex→centroid distance: tens of metres on a city lot, but a large rural or industrial parcel is hundreds.\" Part 3's `frameMovedTo` IS that first vertex (`getCurrentSiteOrigin()` ← `setLtpOriginIfSafe(parcelFrameOrigin(vertices))`, `boundaryProjection.ts:142`), and it rejects any candidate >250 m from it. On any parcel ~500 m across the AREA CENTROID is rejected and the FRAME ORIGIN (sep ≈ 0) is adopted — reinstating the corner-anchored slab the module exists to prevent, with a tolerance smaller than the offset its own doc names as the reason it exists. Part 3 also REMOVES the same-site ceiling outright in the frozen-LTP case: with a boundary committed `setLtpOriginIfSafe` skips forever, so the parcel candidate is ~0 m from `frameMovedTo` and is adopted at ANY distance from `requested` — directly contradicting the shipped arm `scopeAnchor.spec.ts:148` (\"⛔ A DIFFERENT SITE LOSES\"). Neither reversal is declared.\n\n(4) PART 1's `covered` TEST USES THE WRONG HANDLE, SO IT SUPPRESSES EXACTLY THE RESCUE IT EXISTS TO PROVIDE. `contextLoadInFlight` is written ONLY by `loadContextBuildings` (:11842) — it is the BUILDINGS loader's handle. The rescue fires buildings + trees + street life. Whenever a correct buildings load is in flight (the normal case after any parcel commit), `covered` is true and the TREES and STREET-LIFE rescue is suppressed — and those are precisely the layers `site.location-changed` never reloads (:5103 loads buildings alone). Part 1 therefore does not close the gap its own \"ALSO WORTH A ROW\" identifies. Worse, `reseatRescueFiredAt` is one-shot per anchor and cleared only in the project-switch teardown (:18701), so a rescue whose load fails (offline, 429, superseded by an abort) can never be retried for that site — a fresh L-716-class \"can this ever be true again?\" trap, introduced by a fix whose premise is that the current code fails to verify.\n\n(5) PART 2 IS SAFE BUT NEAR-INERT, AND INVERTS ONE SAFETY ORDERING. `formaMassingOrigin` ← `input.originLat/Lon` ← `getFormaOrigin()` = `resolveSiteFrameOrigin(getCurrentSiteOrigin(), storeLoc, geocodeFrame)` (`GISAreaLayout.ts:2703`, `boundaryProjection.ts:106`), which already prefers the LTP origin — the same source `readSiteLocation()` prefers (`CesiumViewport.ts:4671`). The two differ only by staleness, so the inversion is a no-op outside the window (and the existing suite survives it: `CesiumViewportReseatAnchorCurrentSite.test.ts` stubs `readSiteLocation: () => null`). But it makes an APP-LIFETIME module global (`_lastSiteOrigin`, `siteDispatch.ts:657`, the L-676 leak subject) WIN over the viewport field that IS nulled on project switch (:18640) — a deliberate inversion of the ordering that currently shields a cross-project leak, offered without a teardown check.\n\nTHE INVARIANT THE CURRENT CODE IS PROTECTING, and which the fix trades away: \"no layer may be rebuilt at another site\" (L-12964) is enforced by making `reseatAnchorForCurrentSite` a PURE decision that can only ever REFUSE. Part 1 turns it into a side-effecting force-loader called from three layers on every terrain settle — the same shape as the L-12949 fix it says it avoids, differing only in which anchor it force-loads."

    "The 1143 m refusal is the DESIGNED output of any site move >250 m, not a trace of a mis-anchored load: the baked-position memos (contextFarTierState, contextTreesAt, street-life) legitimately hold the previous site until the new load reaches clearContextBuildings() at :12186, and clampTerrainThenReplace is fired at :8285 — before the loads at :8344 — so on a relief city the clamp settles inside that window every time. So \"context missing\" needs a mechanism that stops the NEW, correctly-anchored :8344 load from finishing, not a mechanism that mis-aims it. The three reachable candidates, in order: (a) the load is superseded after clearContextBuildings() has already emptied the scene — the abort check sits AFTER the clear (near ring placed, then `await farGroundSample`, then `if (signal.aborted) return`), so a late supersede (pan refresh, a second commit, the scope slider) leaves no far tier and a null contextFarTierState; (b) the empty-collection branch at :12001 (clearContextBuildings + \"No surrounding building data for this area\") — a genuinely sparse or off-tile bbox at the new parcel; (c) the photoreal branch at :8310, which clears context and never reloads it. Cheapest decisive probe, and it costs one console line: at the moment of the refusal print contextBuildingsAt, siteScopeClipAt, contextFarTierState and currentContextSite() TOGETHER, plus grep the session for every `[CTX-DIAG] context load ABORTED/superseded` and every `[CTX-DIAG] footprints fetched: … for LAT … LON …`. If the LAST surviving load's lat/lon is the new parcel, the anchor is not the fault and the fault is in the supersede/empty path. Separately — and this part of the claim IS right and should get its own row regardless — `site.location-changed` re-anchors only the buildings (:5103); roads, water, parks, landuse, rail, trees and street life are re-anchored ONLY by renderFormaMassing (:8345-8351), so on any path where it does not run those seven layers stay at the previous site.",
    "MEASURE BEFORE FIXING — and the diagnosis's own cheapest probe is the right one, because the code predicts it will falsify the diagnosis. Given the :11966 abort guard, that line will print the NEW parcel on the surviving load, at which point the anchor mechanism is not the fault.\n\nONE REAL, REACHABLE DEFECT DID SURFACE WHILE CHECKING, AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE FIX FROM PART 3: the codebase holds TWO incompatible definitions of \"the site's centre\". `resolveScopeAnchor` deliberately returns the parcel AREA CENTROID (scopeAnchor.ts's ⭐ ruling); `currentContextSite()` returns the parcel FIRST VERTEX (formaMassingOrigin ← getFormaOrigin ← LTP ← parcelFrameOrigin = ring[0]). On the ONE path where a location-changed load is NOT superseded by a renderFormaMassing — the geocode box (`siteGeocodeSearchBox.ts:162`) with a boundary already committed, which is precisely the L-13086 case the anchor was built for — the completed load stamps `contextBuildingsAt` / `contextFarTierState` at the CENTROID while `currentContextSite()` reports the FIRST VERTEX. On any parcel whose centroid is more than RESEAT_ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M (250 m) from its first vertex — the \"large rural or industrial parcel\" scopeAnchor.ts names — `reseatAnchorForCurrentSite` then refuses FOREVER, on every terrain settle, for trees, street life and the far tier, with no successor load anywhere. That is a permanent, self-inflicted refusal loop reachable without any stale-anchor mechanism at all, and it matches \"context missing\" better than the transient this diagnosis describes.\n\nThe fix for it is to UNIFY the two definitions — and to unify them on the CENTROID, which is the ruled answer, by having `currentContextSite()` return `parcelCentroidLonLat(this.committedParcelLonLat) ?? formaMassingOrigin ?? readSiteLocation()`.
